## All-cities menu photo and review inventory — updated November 20, 2026

[Full inventory, research queue and production audit](docs/restaurant-menu-inventory-2026-11-12.md) (one section per round, rounds 26-74 with evidence paths and traps).

**Current verified production checkpoint — November 20, round 96 (USA shellfish photos):** **16,630 photographed / 1,339 reviewed / 55,453 effective menu entries**; remaining **38,823 photo gaps / 54,114 review gaps**. Enabled stored rows: 69,418 item lines (19,750 photographed / 1,350 reviewed). Added **three verified food photos** at [Holbox, Los Angeles](https://www.chronopin.com/pin/6686): Pata de Mula, Almeja Preparada and a distinct shrimp-only Coctel de Camaron. Credited Jeffrey/Foodmento and Ron De Angelis/Los Angeles Times, uploaded to Azure; historical presentation and unspecified serving weights are disclosed. Preserved every other menu/profile field. No new reviews. Complete API readbacks, photo-only convergence, **27 public images**, 11 serialized native excerpts, all **1,478 source quote budgets**, six-review caps and zero pending data operations pass. Hydrated shellfish/cocktail viewer check remains pending because concurrent Chrome sessions repeatedly switched tabs/windows; do not count that interaction check as passed. No unrelated concurrent production changes in this pass. Holbox has 21 remaining photo gaps. Production only; no local database mirror, code deployment, translations or commit. Evidence: `.scrape/menu-research-all-2026-11-13/nov20-round96/`.

**November 19 — San Diego Maps/Yelp passes, then non-US queues (rounds 68-74, about 258 production item lines, all production-only; local not mirrored because local IDs differ; no deploy, translations or commits).** Fresh San Diego inventory at the start: 261 venues, 4,321 items, 1,542 photographed (35.7%), 90 reviewed.
- **Round 68, Google Maps menu tiles, San Diego (~35 lines, 17 venues):** Menu tab dish tiles matched exactly to gap items; works for ordinary menus, almost never for drinks or happy-hour lines. Maps is exhausted for San Diego.
- **Round 69, Yelp Drink galleries, San Diego (60 lines, 33 venues):** the Drink tab returns 50-120 captioned photos per venue; chain branches need the Yelp page's `streetAddress` checked against the saved address (Oggi's Vista/Encinitas and Bluewater Coronado returned the wrong branch).
- **Round 70, Yelp Food tab, deep pages (`start=0..210`), San Diego (59 lines) plus a Phoenix pilot (7 lines):** the earlier passes stopped at `start=180`; deeper pages and the token-subset matcher found far more. Phoenix gave only about 0.4 photos per venue (already mined). A Yelp bot check ("We want to make sure you are not a robot", 403 on search) appeared after about 1,000 requests in one tab; it cleared after the owner opened yelp.com, and the slower pace (4 s searches, 3.5-5.5 s pages, 6 pages) did not retrigger it.
- **Round 71, Japan probe (0 photos):** Maps Menu tiles exist for about 3 of 5 sampled venues but their dishes do not match the saved campaign/hotel/drink lines; Tabelog loads (its "Just a moment" page clears itself) but thumbnails have no dish captions.
- **Round 72, Canada Yelp Food tab (~32 lines, 11 venues; French Montreal/Quebec City not queried), Round 73, Maps tiles Australia/NZ/UK/Ireland (34 lines, 12 venues), Round 74, Maps tiles continental Europe and Monterrey (31 lines, 10 venues).** English and local-language à-la-carte menus match Maps tiles well (about 2 photos per venue); a tile whose name ends in "…" can carry a neighbouring dish's photo, so inspect every one.
- **Method and traps (full detail in rounds 69-74):** read results through the DOM (`document.body.innerHTML='<pre id=o>'` then `get_page_text`) because `javascript_tool` blocks outputs containing photo IDs; use top-level `await`, not an async IIFE; skip the "Popular" badge text when reading a Maps tile's label; fetch Maps tokens (140 characters, at most five per call) and download with plain `curl`; `applyReviewed.py` never replaces an existing photo and errors when an item name is not on that record, so resolve menu label and item name from a live snapshot (`build.py` in `.scrape/menu-research-all-2026-11-13/yelp-food-sd/`); the admin token expires about every 2 hours, refresh with the `admin-login-refresh` skill.
- **Still open (largest first):** Japan (Sapporo 5.9k, Osaka/Tokyo 4.1k each, Nagoya 4.0k, Fukuoka 3.6k, Kyoto 1.2k stored lines) and China/Korea photo gaps, with no dish-labelled source found (hotel course lines, izakaya campaign menus and drink lists have no single dish photo); French-language Montreal and Quebec City venues; tasting-menu-only lines everywhere; the remaining San Diego gaps are generic lines (wine, beer, well drinks, "select" cocktails). ## Production timelines for hardware, drugs, space, vehicles and builds (November 1-2, 2026)

**Done, on production (deployed):** `PinPerf` tables take `display: 'timeline'` (4 columns Date/Milestone/Status/Source plus `stages` and `current`): a stepper and dated milestones, with Source cells shown as `[n]` links to the References panel (a trailing "Source" column on any table is folded into the previous cell). The pin's start bar runs from the earliest to the latest placeable row (`timelineSpan` in `pinPerf.ts`). Labels by kind (`src/lib/pinDateKind.ts`): construction = Groundbreaking/Completion, chip pins = Announced/Commercialization, any other timeline pin keeps Start/End and the bar ends "Final milestone". ## Google organic traffic collapse (read-only investigation, October 29, 2026)

- [ ] **Googlebot stopped crawling around October 7-8; impressions and clicks fell to ~0 from about October 15.** Search Console (28 days to Oct 27): 499 clicks, 26.3K impressions, average position 7.6, nearly all before Oct 15. Crawl stats: ~75K Googlebot requests/day peaked Oct 5-6 (sitemap discovery from Sep 22), then ~0 from about Oct 8; host status "Problems in the past" for www.chronopin.com (robots.txt not available / page could not be reached / 5xx all under 1%). Page indexing: 15.9K indexed (still rising, so not de-indexed), 12.7K not indexed: 10,289 "Excluded by noindex" (the Oct 12 thin-pin gate), 2,162 "Crawled - currently not indexed", 77 blocked by robots.txt, 7 server errors (validation started). Queries are exact-date questions about upcoming events, many non-English (grammys 2027 nominations/nominados, wnba final 2026, dreamforce 2027 dates, met gala 2027 date, windows 11 26h2, patch tuesday november 2026, aldi cora woluwe); topics are mostly already pinned (checked ~25 via /api/pins/search; the search is fuzzy, so thin/noindexed status per pin is unchecked).
  - **Not the cause:** Googlebot UA gets 200 on `/`, a pin page and `/sitemap.xml` (single 17 MB urlset, 9,670 URLs, under limits); robots.txt fine; the cookie gate (Oct 17) came after the drop and skips declared bots. In today's Caddy log Googlebot made ~114 requests, 107 x 200, 4 x 308, 2 x 404, no 429/503.
  - **Likely cause (not proven):** Google lowered its crawl rate while the site was slow/overloaded (Oct 10 502s, `shedding()` caps all Google crawlers at 10 requests/min with 429/503 while on, plus the real-reader API load below). The Caddy log only starts Oct 29 11:48 UTC (capped, recreated), so the Oct 8-10 window cannot be checked.
  - **Today's traffic (Caddy log, ~10 h, 1.33M requests, 98.5K non-bot IPs):** ~69.5K 429s and ~13.8K 503s, almost all browser UAs (shedding is firing); load average 8.6 / 5.4 / 3.8 at 21:29 UTC on the D2s_v5. The big Chinese-ISP ranges (112.90.2.0/24, 120.241.127.0/24, 120.233.111.0/24, 157.148.x, 58.254.x, 14.116.x, 183.2.x; top 50 /24s = 43% of non-bot requests, ~1,000 requests per IP) are **real browsers, not a scraper**: Referer on 479K of 638K, session cookies, paths `/api/users/me`, `/api/live`, `/api/pins/N/weather`, `/api/weather`, `/api/pins/map`, `/api/pins/tag-counts`, `/api/pins/place-counts`, `/api/cultural-days`. Blocking those ranges would cut off real readers. **99.76.225.140 (AWS, `curl/8.1.1`, 10,485 requests, 201/401s on `/api/admin/db/Pin`, `/api/pins/N/factory`, `/api/restaurants/guide`) is Ian's own admin/enrichment tooling** (curl UAs are exempt from the limits); do not block it.
  - **VM is already non-burstable:** `az vm list` shows `Standard_D2s_v5` (resized Oct 18; see the Oct 26 section below). My Oct 29 suggestion to upsize was wrong, from a stale memory note. A bigger size (D4s_v5, about double) is the only remaining hardware option.
  - [x] `.claude/settings.local.json` (gitignored): allow rules and an autoMode entry for read-only `ssh ... 'docker logs docker-caddy-1 ...'` (owner approved Oct 29). Nothing on prod was changed.
  - **Correction (Oct 29):** the Oct 26/27 changes below (IP burst 90, locale-mismatch bucket, tag-count gate, per-cookie `searchRetryAfter`, pin-weather edge cache) are committed and ON PROD: prod's `.deployed-sha` is `3f02593c` and `730bd912`/`92fa50e9` are its ancestors. Their "local, uncommitted, undeployed" wording is stale. Only `002c3188` (docs/data) is undeployed.
  - **Search cohort measured (Caddy log, ~10 h):** 308K `/search` requests = 29% of handler time (167K s); 45,496 IPs, median 3 per IP, top 100 IPs 9.7%; 79% no Referer, 88% Chinese Accept-Language; statuses 307 164K, 200 85K, 403 30K, 429 22K, 503 2.4K; ~40K distinct `q` (tag:/place:/company: label links). The per-cookie budget (burst 30) cannot catch a client making ~3 requests. `/api/pins/map` (44.6K, 41K distinct URLs, from/to in ms) and `tag-counts` with `q` (27.6K distinct) come from the same cohort and are not cacheable; throttling the search page removes them. `/api/pins/place-counts`: 48K requests, one distinct URL (`limit=200`). Caddy redacts the Cookie header in the log, so cookie counts there are unreliable.
  - [x] **Done locally Oct 29, uncommitted, undeployed:** (1) `bareSearchRetryAfter` in `src/server/botLimit.ts` (shared bucket, burst 30, 2/s, only while `shedding()`), applied in `src/proxy.ts` to signed-out, cookie-passing `/search?q=` requests with no Referer, answered with `tooManyRequests`; tests in `botLimit.test.ts` (28 pass), typecheck clean. (2) `/api/pins/place-counts` added to the edge cache (`Docker/edgecache/default.conf` 5 min, `@regional` regex in `Docker/Caddyfile`); `nginx -t` and `caddy validate` pass in the pinned images. Expected: roughly 10-15% less handler time from (1) if it removes half to two thirds of the cohort's 85K renders (plus their map/tag-counts calls), about 2.6% from (2). After deploy: `docker restart docker-caddy-1` and recreate edgecache (bind-mounted configs), check `X-Edge-Cache` on `/api/pins/place-counts?limit=200`, re-count 429s on `/search` and the 5-minute slow-request count. **Deployed Oct 29 (Ian asked) as `c53c21a`**: `.deployed-sha` matches, new Caddy/edgecache configs live without a manual restart, `/api/pins/place-counts?limit=200` returns `X-Edge-Cache: HIT`. Not yet checked: the 429 count on `/search` and the slow-request count after the deploy (the bucket only acts while shedding). The first `npm run deploy` died watching the GitHub run (connection reset); the image had built, and re-running the same deploy finished it.
  - [ ] **Next (needs Ian):** (1) in Search Console resubmit the sitemap and run "Validate fix" on the 7 server-error pages once load is down; (2) reduce per-page-load API cost for real readers (cache or defer weather, tag-counts, place-counts, cultural-days, map, users/me; the Oct 27 weather edge cache and `searchRetryAfter` are still local/undeployed, so a deploy would help; deploy only on request); (3) consider D4s_v5 or a second app instance (see Options below); (4) then enrich noindexed/thin pins that match the real queries (10,289 noindex), and add any missing evergreen "release date / when was X released" pins. Do not expect new content to be noticed until Google's crawl recovers. Google cap while shedding raised from 10 to 60 requests/min on Nov 1 (`GOOGLE_PER_MINUTE`, botLimit.ts). The site footer now links /tags and /companies. Both are in commit fadd760d (swept in by another session's commit), not deployed.

## Prod slowness (read-only check, October 26, 2026)

- [ ] **Prod is CPU-bound on a non-burstable VM; the October 17 resize is already done.** Instance metadata reports `Standard_D2s_v5` (2 vCPUs, booted 2026-10-18 02:01), so the older B2ms/CPU-credit explanation in the Oct 17 note above no longer applies. Snapshot at 14:40 UTC: load 6.48 / 5.43 / 3.71 on 2 cores, CPU 71% user, `docker-app-1` ~86% (the single `next-server` Node thread ~91%), Postgres ~81% (many short queries; none running when sampled), Caddy 17-36% CPU and ~900 MB RSS, memory fine. Caddy log, 5 minutes: 13,395 requests (~45/s) from 1,122 distinct IPs, top IP only 278, 94% claiming Windows Chrome, mostly Chinese carrier addresses (a spread-out crawl; not confirmed as the Oct 17 scraper). `/_next/static` is ~80% of handler seconds (2,559 requests in 3 minutes, all 200, avg 3.1 s, ~25 KB), yet Caddy already serves it from the `next-static` volume (`/srv/assets`, 76 build dirs), so those seconds are Caddy starved of CPU, not Node queueing. A per-IP limit does not touch this traffic.
  - [x] Per-IP burst lowered 100 -> 90 (`IP_BURST` in `src/server/botLimit.ts`; refill still 6/s; tests pass). Local, uncommitted, undeployed.
  - [x] `log_skip @static` added to `Docker/Caddyfile` (static files out of the JSON access log; `caddy validate` OK). Local, uncommitted, undeployed. Compression left as is (not checked whether woff2 is already skipped).
  - [x] Crawler cohort throttled (local, uncommitted, undeployed): the 5-minute log shows ~1,900 page requests from browsers sending `Accept-Language: zh-CN` on `/it`, `/hi`, `/fr`, `/ms` pages (about 1,400 with no Referer; only 62 on `/zh`), mostly searches. `localeMismatch` + `mismatchRetryAfter` in `src/server/botLimit.ts` (wired in `src/proxy.ts`): signed-out GETs by a Chinese browser on a non-Chinese language path share one bucket (burst 20, 1/s) **only while `shedding()` is on**, else a plain 429. Not geo-blocking; a real Chinese reader on a French page is only affected while the server is overloaded. Tests added; typecheck clean. After deploy, re-run the Caddy cohort count and watch for 429s on `/it`, `/hi`, `/fr`, `/ms`.
  - [x] Postgres load found and gated (local, uncommitted, undeployed): 25 samples of `pg_stat_activity` (read-only, ~1 minute) put 72 of 112 active samples (64%, up to 20 s each, 107 of 112 on CPU) on the search tag-cloud count (`searchTagCounts`, the `WITH "hits" AS (SELECT DISTINCT tg.name ...` join); 12 on the confidence-band query. `cachedTagCounts` is already `use cache` for hours but keyed by query text, and the swarm's searches are all distinct. New `src/server/tagCountGate.ts` (`withTagCountSlot`, tests in `tagCountGate.test.ts`): an uncached count runs only when not shedding and at most 2 at once (waits up to 3 s for a slot); otherwise it throws `TagCountBusy` out of the `use cache` function (so nothing is cached) and `searchPageTagCounts` shows no cloud. Cached clouds are unaffected. Not yet done: an `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` of the query on prod and an index or precomputed counts for `PinTag`; enabling `pg_stat_statements` (needs a Postgres restart). After deploy, re-sample `pg_stat_activity` and check the load.
  - [ ] Options not yet chosen: (1) second app instance behind Caddy `lb_policy round_robin` (per-process rate-limit buckets, caches and background jobs run twice); (2) (done above) ; (3) D4s_v5 (4 vCPUs, about double the cost). Re-run the Postgres active-query check with a quoting-safe script (scratchpad `q.sql` piped over SSH) when load is high.

## Bot traffic, rate limits and heavy user plan (updated October 20, 2026)

**Deployed to production (release `aece0bfd`, October 17 evening), after an overload that took prod down for about an hour.** Cause: link prefetches (about ten page renders per page view) plus a distributed scraper (~500 IPs fetching one or two pins each, 62% with no Referer) on a 2-vCPU VM. Fixes live: `Link` defaults to `prefetch={false}`; cookie gate on pin pages for non-bot clients without cookies (`src/server/cookieGate.ts`: 307 to `?cg=1` setting a `cg` cookie, 403 for cookieless clients; right after it, 80% of the IPs given a 307 never came back); per-IP/user request limit (`ipRetryAfter` in `src/server/botLimit.ts`: free burst 50, refill 2/s, prefetch costs 0.25; heavy plan burst 1,200, 20/s; admins and named bots exempt; over the limit browsers see `/heavy-use?limited=1` as a 429); `busyness` Chromium scrapes skip while the server sheds load; weather-route 502s are cached 5 minutes and logged once. Heavy user plan ($5 for 30 days or $4/month, no ads; `HeavyUserPlan` migration 0191, `/api/billing/heavy/*`, webhook branch on `heavyUserId`) is deployed but unusable until Stripe is configured; its ad (`heavyuser`, 16 locales) is paused (`Ad.active = false`). Caddy now writes a JSON access log (client IP, user agent, path, status; cookies and Authorization dropped) and its container log is capped at 2 GB (`max-size 50m`, `max-file 40`, applied by recreating the container). The emergency Caddy 204 rule for prefetches was removed again.

- [ ] **Commit the Caddyfile edit** that removes the emergency 204 prefetch rule (`Docker/Caddyfile`); it is already live on the VM. Note: after a deploy replaces `Docker/Caddyfile`, the caddy container keeps the old file (single-file bind mount, new inode) until `docker restart docker-caddy-1`; a log-option change needs `docker compose up -d caddy`.
- [ ] **Re-measure after about 24 hours of access log** (log started with the Caddy restart on October 17; it was recreated at about 21:15 UTC for the size cap, so history restarts there): split by hour into quiet and busy periods; recheck where 502s and slow responses cluster, per-IP request rates, the cookie gate (307/403 counts, share of pin requests without a Referer, how many IPs return with the cookie) and `/api/main` (p50 570 ms), `/api/main/day` (350 ms), `/api/ads` (200 ms) and `/api/pins/highlights` (550 ms). Earlier samples: ~2,000 distinct IPs, mostly real `/zh` timeline readers (Chinese carrier ranges, Windows Chrome), plus the pin-page scraper; a few single-IP /16s (111.57, 221.131, 60.10, 222.79, 112.17) to watch. No Singapore traffic seen. Do not block whole /16s.
- [ ] **The app's single Node thread is saturated by server rendering; the homepage is the heaviest page (profiled October 17, evening).** Two CPU profiles of the running app (Node inspector on 127.0.0.1:9229 inside `docker-app-1`; script pattern: connect over WebSocket, `Profiler.start`, wait, `Profiler.stop`, aggregate self time and look for stretches without idle samples) showed 61 s of busy time in a 60 s window, i.e. the event loop never idles; the time is React server render/serialisation in Next's app-page runtime (`eQ` alone 17%, GC 4%), not database code, Chromium or background jobs. Effect: any request, even `/robots.txt`, is either fast (0.2-1 s) or waits 30-40+ s in the queue (sequential probes from the VM: 7 of 30 timed out at 40 s). Per route, one at a time: `/about` 0.38 s, `/search` 0.48 s, `/map` 0.29 s, a pin page 0.9 s, but `/` 2 s when warm and 36-49 s in other runs, with a 1.1 MB response (about 320 KB of RSC payload, 46 `<article>` cards, 298 pin links to 69 pins, 196 `<img>`, 243 `<svg>`). `HomeTimeline` calls `connection()` so it renders per request, and `timelinePage` is `use cache` for minutes keyed by cursor, posting window, locale and time zone. **Done locally, uncommitted and undeployed (October 17, late): (2) the home page is cheaper.** The first render takes 15 pins either side of now instead of 25 (`HOME_FIRST_PAGE` in `src/app/[lang]/(timeline)/page.tsx`, `initialSize` in `TimelineQuery`; later pages stay 25), and the signed-out home page HTML is kept in memory per language and time zone (`src/server/homeCache.ts`, wired into `src/proxy.ts`): fresh for 60 s, then served stale for up to 10 minutes while one background render refreshes it, one render at a time per key; signed-in visitors, requests with a query string, RSC/prefetch fetches and non-document requests render for themselves. The copy is made by the proxy asking its own server on 127.0.0.1:`PORT` with a per-process secret header (`x-home-fill`) that skips the bot, limit and cookie-gate checks, so `PORT` must be the port the app listens on (9000 in Docker). Checked on the local dev server: first request 0.78 s, repeats 5-18 ms and byte-identical, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `?posted=all` and signed-in cookies bypass it. Deployed as `25a838a8` (October 17, late, clean deploy: one app container, `.deployed-sha` advanced). Prod after it: `/` first render 17 s under load, then 0.43-0.72 s per copy (791 KB, 29 cards, was 1.1 MB and 46); `/zh` 9.5 s then 0.6 s; Caddy-log average handler time per route fell for pin pages 8.3 s to 2.3 s and for `/` 11.2 s to 4.1 s. Still seen after it: single requests to cheap routes stalling 15-30 s (`/about` 30 s, `/robots.txt` 21 s) at about 15 requests/s with pin pages still 41% of handler time (about 5.6 pin renders/s) and the app near 160% CPU, so the second instance (option 1) is still needed. **Correction, same night: it would not help, the VM is CPU-throttled.** `Standard_B2ms` is burstable and `az monitor` shows `CPU Credits Remaining` at about 1.4-2.5 for six hours (the bank holds hundreds), `Percentage CPU` flat at 29%, guest CPU 48-67% busy, which matches the B2ms baseline (about 60% of the VM, from memory, not read from Azure); the idle time inside the guest is not usable. A second instance would only share the same cap. Real fix: a non-burstable size. westus2 pay-as-you-go Linux, 730 h: B2ms about $61/mo (now), D2ls_v5 or F2s_v2 about $62 (4 GB only), D2as_v6 about $66 (needs NVMe disk controller; this VM is SCSI, so skip), D2s_v5 or D2as_v4 about $70 (8 GB, 2 full vCPUs). Quota is free for all (DSv5, DASv4, FSv2 families 0 of 20 used); the VM is Gen2, public IP `20.109.175.187` is Static (survives deallocation). Resize = `az vm deallocate` then `az vm resize --size Standard_D2s_v5` then `az vm start`, a few minutes of downtime; run it when traffic is low and check `docker ps` after (restart policy brings the containers back). docs/deploy-azure.md still says the $50 monthly credit is the spending cap while B2ms alone is about $61: check the subscription's real budget before resizing. Not yet checked: hydration of a served copy in a browser. Effects to expect: a new pin appears on the anonymous home page up to a minute later; `serverNow` in a stale copy can be up to 10 minutes old. Each card is still about 15 KB of HTML (about 4.6 `<img>` with long srcsets and about 100 class attributes), so slimming the card is the next saving. Remaining options, in order: (1) use both vCPUs: Node runs on one core, so run a second app instance behind Caddy `lb_policy round_robin` (mind per-process state: rate-limit buckets, caches, background jobs run twice) or resize the VM; (2) shrink the first homepage (fewer cards or lazy rows) and cache the anonymous render; (3) `DetermineComponentFrameRoot` (React component-stack capture) is only a side cost: 1.7% of CPU over a 40 s profile (9% in one busy stretch). Its call chain is React's `onError` handler fired from abort/reject paths (no render errors appear in the app log), which fits renders being aborted for clients that gave up (about 800 abandoned requests, status 0, in 3 minutes in the Caddy log); each abort walks the component tree to build a stack. It is a feedback loop, not a cause: queueing makes clients abandon, aborts cost CPU. Shortening the queue fixes it. Also seen: a tag-count query (`WITH "hits" AS (SELECT DISTINCT ...` from `searchTagCounts`) running 12 s, and deploy warm-up tag-count calls taking 27-52 s each.
- [x] **Free limits raised for every browser (October 20, 2026).** The 24-hour Caddy log showed about 19,300 page 429s across 3,842 IPs, mostly crawlers (216.73.217.12, 74.7.241.52, an 18.97.9.x block), but about 908 browser-looking IPs with a Referer (1,524 requests; 1,158 on one macOS Safari user agent) were also limited; the 403s (8,224 pages, 1,169 pin pages, 1,157 of those with no Referer) were cookieless Windows Chrome, i.e. the scraper; the 502s were almost all `/api/pins/N/weather` (Open-Meteo quota, stopped about 12:00 UTC); 1,400 page 503s fell between 05:00 and 07:00 UTC. Limits then: per address burst 150, 6/s (was 50, 2/s); per cookie pin pages burst 90, one every 1.3 s (was 30, one every 4 s). **Code on October 27 has per address burst 90, 6/s (`IP_BURST`, `IP_PER_SECOND`) and per cookie pin pages burst 90, 0.75/s (`PIN_BURST`, `PIN_PER_SECOND`); the 150 was lowered later.** heavy plan unchanged (1,200, 20/s). A first version relaxed only macOS Safari (`isMacSafari`); it was removed when the same figures became the default (`src/server/botLimit.ts`, `src/proxy.ts`, tests updated). Risk: a scraper with a browser user agent that keeps cookies now gets 3x the budget; if it returns, lower `PIN_BURST` first. Local, not yet deployed.
- [x] **Over the pin limit, signed-out visitors go to sign in (October 20, 2026, deployed and checked on prod).** `signInForMore` in `src/proxy.ts` sends a browser page request over the per-cookie pin limit to `/login?limited=1&redirect=<pin>` (307, `Retry-After`); other requests still get a plain 429. The login form (`LoginForm.tsx`) and the heavy-use page say higher browsing limits are available for signed-in users (keys `account.pinLimitNotice`, `account.higherLimits`). Signed-in users skip the per-cookie pin limit but keep their own per-user request budget. Verified on prod with one cookie: pins 1-3 returned 200, pin 28 onward redirected to login (the first redirect was somewhere before 28; not pinned down).
- [x] **Heavy-use page translated into all 16 languages (October 20, 2026).** `heavy.*` keys in every `src/lib/i18n/messages/*.ts`; `page.tsx` uses `getT` + `generateMetadata`, `HeavyPlans.tsx` uses `useT`. Prices stay in USD. The translations are unreviewed: have native speakers check ar, hi, th, vi and the FAQ cancellation answers.
- [ ] **Intermittent slowness, read-only check October 27, 2026 (22:30 UTC, nothing changed).** VM is now `Standard_D2s_v5` (throttling gone), load about 1, Postgres idle, `docker-edgecache-1` running. Last hour of Caddy log: ~111k requests from ~7,900 IPs (~32/s), 91% Windows Chrome UA with a Referer on 86%, calling `/api/users/me`, `/api/live`, weather: mostly real `/zh` readers, declared crawlers only 40-80 requests per 5 minutes each, top IP 722 requests. 99% of requests fine (p50 0.02 s, p99 2.3 s); 899 (0.8%) took over 5 s, 437 abandoned by the client, clustered in two 5-minute windows (21:40, 21:50 UTC) that were not the busiest; both had ~2,300 `/<lang>/search?q=tag:"..."` requests from ~800 IPs (~3 each, `cg=1`, so the cookie gate passes them) plus weather and map spikes. App container ~105% CPU = one core; the second vCPU is idle. Per-IP limit (burst 90) cannot catch ~3 requests per IP. Options: (1) second app instance behind Caddy round-robin (per-process buckets, home cache, background jobs run twice); (2) charge gated `?q=` searches to the per-cookie budget like pin pages; (3) cache anonymous `/search?q=` and `/api/pins/N/weather` (15.6k/hour) for a few minutes. Needs an explicit deploy request. **Done locally October 27, uncommitted, undeployed:** (2) `searchRetryAfter` in `src/server/botLimit.ts` (burst 30, one every 2 s per gate cookie, prefetch 0.25) applied in `src/proxy.ts` to signed-out `/search?q=` requests, over the limit sent to sign-in like pins; (3) pin weather `/api/pins/N/weather` now goes through the edge cache (`Docker/edgecache/default.conf`: 200 15 min, 204 10 min; `Docker/Caddyfile` `@regional` is one `path_regexp`). Search pages themselves are not cached (long-tail unique queries would rarely hit). After deploy: `docker restart docker-caddy-1` and recreate edgecache (config bind mounts), then check `X-Edge-Cache` on a weather URL and the 5-minute slow-request count.

# Left over: link wikis for prod

As of 2026-10-04 (sizing refreshed 2026-10-11). Prod's Anthropic key has no credit, so its link wikis are written here and sent over the API (docs/okf/api/source-wikis.md, memory note prebuilt-wikis).

- **Sizing, 2026-10-11 (the live backlog):** 12,695 pending sources on 4,527 pins (12,202 pending + 493 failed with tries spent; 12,035 web, 492 pdf, 109 youtube, 58 podcast, 1 tweet; 6,735 already have fetched text; pin ids 1-7669, mostly 3000-7000). Biggest hosts: en.wikipedia.org 1,345, sec.gov 367 (long filings, costly), pennrose.com 304, myanimelist.net 199, kalshi.com 192 (JS pages), anilist.co 188, cnbc.com 179, themoviedb.org 153, then grammy.com, espn.com, rottentomatoes.com, fifa.com, federalreserve.gov, boxofficemojo.com at about 100 each. Cost measured on the 431-wiki local-sync batch: about 6k tokens per wiki (20 agents, 2.6M tokens), roughly 75% of links readable, so the whole backlog is about 55M tokens, 4.5 h of local page fetching (about 12 min per 550 links) and about 2 h of gated pushing (25 per request, 15 s apart). Not one session's work. **Tranches, best value first:** A = links of the newest ~500 pins (ids 7000+ and the near-term event window), ~830 links, ~5M tokens; B = en.wikipedia.org only, 1,345 links, ~6M tokens; skip sec.gov, kalshi.com, anilist/MAL stubs and login-walled hosts (dianping). Wikis only feed pin summaries and the pending badge, so older pins gain little. Run each tranche as export `--links FILE` (page text on the Mac), writers per batch of ~20 jobs sized by bytes, `validate`, `push` (it pauses 15 s between requests), then re-pull the pending list; retire the unreadable ones with `status failed, attempts 3` (PATCH /api/admin/db/Source/<id>) so they stop showing as pending. The 2026-10-11 local-sync batch already did this for its own 198 pins: 431 wikis pushed, 25 placeholders reset to pending, 145 unreadable links retired (`.scrape/prod-batches/local-sync-2026-10-10/`).
- **~8,600 links still pending on prod (2026-10-04 figure, superseded by the line above)** (`GET /api/admin/source-wikis` for the live count). Done so far: a pilot of 24 (pins 5884-5891), wave 1 (145 links, pins down to about 5749) and wave 2 (143 links, pins 5923-5966), 312 wikis in all, every one ready with its summary left as it was. The backfill runs newest pins first in waves of 150 (the chosen scope); wave 3 starts from the next-newest link in `GET /api/admin/source-wikis`. Unreadable so far (need the browser or another source): businesswire.com, Bloomberg, Zacks and airdatanews bot checks, a JSE PDF and a PDF-only newsroom card. The count does not fall fast, because pins other sessions post to prod without `sourceWikis` add new pending links (8,599 after the pilot, 8,632 after wave 1, 8,630 after wave 2). A session writes about 9k tokens per wiki, so the whole backlog is too big for one session. Cheaper: top up credit and run `wiki:sync` on prod, or write only each pin's main source link.
- **To continue:** `npm run wiki:prod -- export --pending --out DIR --limit 150` (server order is oldest first, so for newest first export a `--links FILE` sorted by `max(pinIds)` descending), have agents write `DIR/results/wikis/<n>.json` from `DIR/wikis/<n>.json` per `DIR/prompts.md`, then `npm run wiki:prod -- push --dir DIR --by claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-5`. Links the export could not read land in `DIR/blocked/`: businesswire.com (bot check), JSE PDFs (403) and a few thin pages need the browser or another source.
- **New pins:** `wiki:prod export --drafts DRAFTS/`, answer the jobs, `wiki:prod attach --dir DIR --drafts DRAFTS/`, then post as usual, so the pin carries its wikis (`sourceWikis`) and prod runs no wiki work.
- **Only the wiki step is skipped on prod.** Podcast cross-checks, sentiment, stocks, awards and the other save listeners still run on prod for every post; the rest of "process locally, post the final pin" is still open.
- Wikis pushed with `--rewrite` bump the version and put every citing pin's summary behind its links; leave it off for a backfill.


# Cleanview energy and data-center pins — remainder (updated 2026-10-18)

Batch folder (gitignored): `.scrape/prod-batches/cleanview-2026-10-14/`. Source: cleanview.co's public tables (100 rows per tab, A-Z) plus its public state pages (largest planned data centers, solar farms and batteries per state, and solar/battery "coming online soon"), scraped to `state-leads-all.json` (1,424 leads). Pins are @EnergyDesk, tags `Cleanview` + `Data Centers` / `Solar` / `Storage` (+ `Battery`, status, developer, state, grid, `Signed Tenant` only for a named or signed data-center tenant).

**Done:** 880 pins on prod and 880 on local (`posted-prod.json` / `posted-local.json` map draft key to id) across Rounds 1–60, with 100% start-date (`utcStartDateTime`) and `dateConfidence` reconciliation completed on 2026-10-18. Rounds 1-6: 188 pins (prod 15643-15881, local 15519-15758) with ja/zh/ko. 2026-10-16: 13 promoted held drafts (prod 15933-15945, local 17038-17050), 36 new data-center campuses (prod 15979-16014, local 17084-17119), Aramis and Alcazar updated (second amendment to 1 Apr 2027, Resolution 569 "Defeated in Committee"; Alcazar 3/4 NYISO claim unverified, left out), five existing pins refreshed from improved drafts (Astoria, Angelina, Van Nuys, Rt 5, Althea). 2026-10-17 to 2026-10-19: 641 state-page clean energy and data center pins across Rounds 7–60 (prod 16299–18208) live with 3 verified USGS media layers per pin. All read back against drafts, including `utcStartDateTime` and `dateConfidence`: 0 mismatches. Tools: `post_local.py <keys>`, `run_prod.py` (stops at first failure; drop a 403 image and rerun), `validate.py`, `adm.py`, `fix_start_dates.py`, `fix_all_rounds_dates.py`; `BRIEF.md` has the drafting rules. **Save trap:** `PUT /api/pins/:id` replaces media wholesale; always send the full `media` array (omitting it wiped AES Rt 5's photo until restored). Check media with `GET /api/admin/db/PinMedium?pinId=`.
